    1|       |//! Constrained topology and Delaunay triangulation.
    2|       |//!
    3|       |//! The implementation handles exact incremental Delaunay point sets, planarizes
    4|       |//! crossing constraints with exact Steiner points, recovers protected edges by
    5|       |//! flipping or exact cavity retriangulation, and re-legalizes unprotected
    6|       |//! edges. Topology-only consumers can omit empty-circle quality work and use a
    7|       |//! deterministic lexicographic triangulation. The production paths own their
    8|       |//! topology implementation and have no external triangulation dependency.
    9|       |

  249|       |/// Triangulate an exact point set using a deterministic BRIO-style batch order.
  250|       |///
  251|       |/// The input point buffer and every triangle index still refer to the caller's
  252|       |/// original order. Only the internal Bowyer--Watson insertion schedule changes:
  253|       |/// a deterministic randomized hierarchy supplies successively larger rounds,
  254|       |/// and a median spatial traversal improves locality within each round. This is
  255|       |/// useful for large batches whose caller order is unrelated to geometry.
  256|       |///
  257|       |/// For cocircular point sets, Delaunay triangulations are not unique, so this
  258|       |/// function can choose different valid diagonals than [`delaunay`]. Callers
  259|       |/// that require the historical insertion-order tie topology should use
  260|       |/// [`delaunay`].

  294|       |///
  295|       |/// The constrained path appends exact intersection vertices for proper
  296|       |/// constraint crossings, normalizes constraints into internal PSLG
  297|       |/// subsegments, and recovers those subsegments with exact edge flips or exact
  298|       |/// cavity retriangulation.

  300|       |/// Closed polygon-with-hole inputs are routed through the boundary-preserving

  302|       |/// triangulated over their convex hull, with protected subsegments excluded
  303|       |/// from local Delaunay flips. Local legality requires every unprotected
  304|       |/// interior edge to satisfy the exact empty-circle test.

  316|       |///

  319|       |/// either side of every protected cycle remains triangulated. This is the

  325|       |/// authored constraints still require exact intersection insertion.

  345|       |/// Triangulate a planar straight-line graph over its complete convex hull
  346|       |/// without imposing a Delaunay quality policy.
  347|       |///
  348|       |/// Every input point is inserted with exact orientation predicates, then every
  349|       |/// protected PSLG edge is recovered through the same exact flip/cavity path as
  350|       |/// [`constrained_delaunay_convex_hull`]. The result covers both sides of closed
  351|       |/// interior cycles. Unlike the Delaunay entry point, unprotected diagonals are
  352|       |/// not legalized with empty-circle predicates. This is the appropriate path
  353|       |/// for topology-only consumers such as surface arrangements.
  354|       |///
  355|       |/// Constraints must already be planarized: they may not cross, overlap, or
  356|       |/// contain an input point other than an endpoint. Every returned triangle is
  357|       |/// strictly positively oriented in the caller's coordinate axes, and every
  358|       |/// input constraint is present as a triangulation edge. Unprotected edges follow
  359|       |/// greedy endpoint-pair order: each admitted diagonal avoids protected edges
  360|       |/// and interior input vertices. Identical protected cells with the same point
  361|       |/// set and relative vertex order therefore receive the same triangle sets,
  362|       |/// independently of surrounding cells and constraint order. Because this checked
  363|       |/// entry point never inserts Steiner points, the returned triangles index the
  364|       |/// caller's point slice directly. The active policy and aggregate certainty
  365|       |/// govern every predicate used to establish those postconditions.
  366|  3.32k|pub fn constrained_triangulation_convex_hull(
  367|  3.32k|    context: &TriangulationContext,
  368|  3.32k|    points: &[ExactPoint],
  369|  3.32k|    constraints: &[Constraint],
  370|  3.32k|) -> Result<TriangulationOutcome<Vec<Triangle>>> {
  371|  3.32k|    constrained_triangulation_convex_hull_with_evaluator(
  372|  3.32k|        PredicateEvaluator::new(context),
  373|  3.32k|        points,
  374|  3.32k|        constraints,
  375|       |    )
  376|  3.32k|}

  899|    624|fn brio_insertion_order(evaluator: &PredicateEvaluator, points: &[Point2]) -> Result<Vec<usize>> {
  900|       |    // A minimum first-round population avoids spending exact spatial-sort
  901|       |    // comparisons on many tiny buckets. Above that floor, splitmix-assigned
  902|       |    // levels produce the geometrically growing rounds of a biased randomized
  903|       |    // insertion order without adding an RNG or making results process-seeded.
  904|       |    const MIN_FIRST_ROUND: usize = 16;
  905|    624|    let mut highest_level = 0_usize;
  906|    652|    while (points.len() >> (highest_level + 1)) >= MIN_FIRST_ROUND {
  907|     28|        highest_level += 1;
  908|     28|    }
  909|       |
  910|    624|    let mut rounds = vec![Vec::new(); highest_level + 1];
  911|  8.71k|    for point in 0..points.len() {
                               ^624
  912|  8.71k|        let level = (splitmix64(point as u64).trailing_zeros() as usize).min(highest_level);
  913|  8.71k|        rounds[level].push(point);
  914|  8.71k|    }
  915|       |
  916|    624|    let mut order = Vec::with_capacity(points.len());
  917|    652|    for round in rounds.iter_mut().rev() {
                               ^624              ^624
  918|    652|        spatial_median_order(evaluator, points, round, false)?;
                                                                           ^0
  919|    652|        order.append(round);
  920|       |    }
  921|    624|    Ok(order)
  922|    624|}

  949|  4.84k|fn select_spatial_nth(

  979|  24.3k|            }

  980|       |        }

  984|  2.97k|            std::cmp::Ordering::Greater => upper = split,

    1|       |//! Exact constraint recovery with structural or Delaunay legalization.
    2|       |//!
    3|       |//! The public [`crate::cdt`] module owns API shape and result records; this
    4|       |//! module keeps the incremental segment-insertion machinery local. The
    5|       |//! implementation starts from the exact Delaunay triangulation, planarizes
    6|       |//! crossing constraints into exact Steiner vertices, recovers each protected
    7|       |//! subsegment by flipping crossed unconstrained edges or retriangulating its
    8|       |//! exact cavity, then re-legalizes only unconstrained edges. Correctness
    9|       |//! reduces to complete convex-hull coverage and local Delaunay checks on
   10|       |//! unprotected edges; exact predicate ownership stays in the evaluator/predicate
   11|       |//! layer.

 2133|      3|    fn exact_normal_positive() -> Real {
 2134|      3|        let root_two = Real::from(2).sqrt().unwrap();
 2135|      3|        let root_two_over_pi = (root_two.clone() / Real::pi()).unwrap();
 2136|      3|        let half = (Real::from(1) / Real::from(2)).unwrap();
 2137|      3|        let shared_offset = root_two.clone() * Real::from(3) + half;
 2138|      3|        let contact = (((root_two.clone() * Real::from(4) - shared_offset.clone()) * Real::pi())
 2139|      3|            * root_two_over_pi.clone()
 2140|      3|            / Real::from(4))
 2141|      3|        .unwrap();
 2142|      3|        let domain = (((root_two * Real::from(2) - shared_offset) * Real::pi()) * root_two_over_pi
 2143|      3|            / Real::from(4))
 2144|      3|        .unwrap()
 2145|      3|            + Real::from(1);
 2146|      3|        contact - domain + Real::from(2).powi_i64(-3000).unwrap()
 2147|      3|    }

    1|       |//! Exact validation helpers for Delaunay and constrained-Delaunay topology.
    2|       |//!
    3|       |//! These routines intentionally stay separate from insertion code. They are
    4|       |//! useful in tests and downstream debug checks, and they document the invariants
    5|       |//! maintained by the current port: protected PSLG edges are present in the
    6|       |//! output triangulation, and every unprotected interior edge can be checked with
    7|       |//! the same empty-circle predicate used by Delaunay insertion.

                                                                                                       ^0

   26|      3|            reason: "triangulation does not cover the convex hull",
   27|      3|        });
   28|  1.77k|    }
   29|  1.77k|    validate_local_delaunay(evaluator, points, &edge_uses, &[])
   30|  1.78k|}
   31|       |
   32|       |/// Return whether `triangles` form one complete triangulation of the convex
   33|       |/// hull of every input point.
   34|       |///
   35|       |/// This catches finite-supertriangle artifacts that local empty-circle checks
   36|       |/// cannot see: missing wedges appear as a concave boundary even though every
   37|       |/// retained interior edge is locally legal.
   38|  7.45k|pub(crate) fn triangulates_convex_hull(
   39|  7.45k|    evaluator: &PredicateEvaluator,
   40|  7.45k|    points: &[ExactPoint],

   43|  7.45k|    let edge_uses = sorted_edge_uses(triangles);
   44|  7.45k|    triangulates_convex_hull_with_edge_uses(evaluator, points, triangles, &edge_uses)
   45|  7.45k|}

   47|  14.8k|fn triangulates_convex_hull_with_edge_uses(
   48|  14.8k|    evaluator: &PredicateEvaluator,

   50|  14.8k|    triangles: &[Triangle],
   51|  14.8k|    edge_uses: &[EdgeUse],
   52|  14.8k|) -> Result<bool> {
   53|  14.8k|    if triangles.is_empty() {
   54|      9|        return points_are_collinear(evaluator, points);
   55|  14.8k|    }
   56|       |
   57|  14.8k|    let mut used = vec![false; points.len()];
   58|   111k|    for triangle in triangles {
                                  ^14.8k
   59|   333k|        if triangle.iter().any(|&vertex| vertex >= points.len()) {
                         ^111k           ^111k
   60|      3|            return Ok(false);
   61|   111k|        }
   62|   333k|        for &vertex in triangle {
                                     ^111k
   63|   333k|            used[vertex] = true;
   64|   333k|        }
   65|       |    }
   66|   109k|    if used.iter().any(|&is_used| !is_used) {
                     ^14.8k      ^14.8k
   67|      9|        return Ok(false);
   68|  14.8k|    }
   69|       |
   70|  14.8k|    let mut boundary = Vec::new();
   71|  14.8k|    let mut parents = (0..triangles.len()).collect::<Vec<_>>();
   72|  14.8k|    let mut edge_count = 0_usize;
   73|  14.8k|    let mut start = 0;
   74|   221k|    while start < edge_uses.len() {

    1|       |//! Caller-certified collinear point sets for constrained triangulation.
    2|       |
    3|       |use crate::context::TriangulationCertainty;
    4|       |use crate::error::{Error, Result};
    5|       |use crate::types::Point2;
    6|       |use hyperlimit::{Certainty, Escalation, PredicateOutcome, PredicatePolicy, Sign};
    7|       |
    8|       |/// Sets of point indices that the caller has proved collinear.
    9|       |///
   10|       |/// Each line names indices into the point slice of one triangulation whose
   11|       |/// points lie on one straight line, by a certificate the caller holds, such as
   12|       |/// the construction that placed them there. Given these facts, a
   13|       |/// triangulation takes the orientation of any three points of one line as
   14|       |/// zero instead of evaluating the determinant, and decides only their order
   15|       |/// along it by exact comparisons. The facts are trusted: a set that is not
   16|       |/// collinear gives wrong topology. A point may lie on several lines, and a set
   17|       |/// of fewer than three points states nothing.
   18|       |///
   19|       |/// The certainty is that of the weakest evidence behind the facts. Facts that
   20|       |/// consumed the approximate terminal are used only under `APPROXIMATE_512`,
   21|       |/// and using one makes the outcome approximate; under `STRICT` they are
   22|       |/// ignored and every determinant is evaluated.
   23|       |#[derive(Clone, Debug, Eq, PartialEq)]
   24|       |pub struct CollinearPointFacts {
   25|       |    memberships: Vec<(usize, u32)>,
   26|       |    lines: u32,
   27|       |    certainty: TriangulationCertainty,
   28|       |}

   87|       |/// Lookup of [`CollinearPointFacts`] by point reference for one triangulation
   88|       |/// of one borrowed point slice.
   89|       |///
   90|       |/// Triangulation algorithms pass references into the caller's slice to the
   91|       |/// operation's predicate evaluator. The slice stays borrowed for the whole

   90|       |/// Validate and triangulate one simple ring like [`triangulate_simple_ring`],

   93|       |/// `apex` indexes `vertices` and must be a ring vertex. Ear clipping tries the

   96|       |/// it and only the pockets it cannot see are cut into other triangles. The

  888|      0|                Err(error) => return Err(error),
  889|       |            }
  890|       |        }
  891|    185|        positions.insert(insert_at, position);
  892|       |    }
  893|       |
  894|     56|    Ok(positions)

 1442|   105k|    let next = ring[(cursor + 1) % len];

 1445|       |    // The exact predicate here is the convexity gate; containment below rejects

 1736|      3|    fn unresolved_bridge_priority_preserves_exact_triangulation() {
 1737|      3|        let sine = Real::e().sin();
 1738|      3|        let cosine = Real::e().cos();
 1739|      3|        let zero =
 1740|      3|            crate::test_uncertainty::terminal_zero(&sine * &sine + &cosine * &cosine - Real::one());
 1741|      3|        let hole = [
 1742|      3|            Point2::new(zero.clone(), Real::zero()),
 1743|      3|            exact_point(0, 2),
 1744|      3|            exact_point(2, 0),
 1745|      3|        ];
 1746|      3|        let context = TriangulationContext::new(hyperlimit::PredicatePolicy::STRICT);
 1747|      3|        assert!(matches!(
                              ^0
 1748|      3|            hyperlimit::compare_point2_lexicographic(
 1749|      3|                &hole[0],
 1750|      3|                &hole[1],
 1751|      3|                context.predicate_policy()
 1752|      3|            ),
 1753|       |            hyperlimit::PredicateOutcome::Unknown { .. }
 1754|       |        ));
 1755|      9|        for shift in 0..3 {
                                   ^3
 1756|     18|            for reversed in [false, true] {
                                          ^9
 1757|     18|                let mut vertices = vec![
 1758|     18|                    exact_point(-10, -10),
 1759|     18|                    exact_point(10, -10),
 1760|     18|                    exact_point(0, 12),
 1761|       |                ];
 1762|     18|                let mut ring = (0..3)
 1763|     54|                    .map(|i| hole[(i + shift) % 3].clone())
                                   ^18
 1764|     18|                    .collect::<Vec<_>>();
 1765|     18|                if reversed {
 1766|      9|                    ring.reverse();
 1767|      9|                }
 1768|     18|                vertices.extend(ring);
 1769|     18|                for _ in 0..2 {
 1770|     36|                    let outcome = triangulate(&context, &vertices, &[3]).unwrap();
 1771|     36|                    assert_eq!(outcome.certainty, crate::TriangulationCertainty::Certified);
 1772|     36|                    let rings = rings_from_hole_indices(&vertices, &[3]).unwrap();
 1773|     36|                    assert!(
 1774|     36|                        triangles_match_input_boundary(
 1775|     36|                            &PredicateEvaluator::new(&context),
 1776|     36|                            &vertices,
 1777|     36|                            &rings,
 1778|     36|                            &outcome.value
 1779|       |                        )
 1780|     36|                        .unwrap()
 1781|       |                    );
 1782|     36|                    let twice_area =
 1783|     36|                        outcome
 1784|     36|                            .value
 1785|     36|                            .as_chunks::<3>()
 1786|     36|                            .0
 1787|     36|                            .iter()
 1788|    216|                            .fold(Real::zero(), |area, tri| {
                                           ^36  ^36
 1789|    648|                                let [a, b, c] = [tri[0], tri[1], tri[2]].map(|i| &vertices[i]);
                                                   ^216^216^216 ^216^216                    ^216
 1790|    216|                                assert_eq!(
 1791|    216|                                    hyperlimit::orient2(a, b, c, context.predicate_policy())
 1792|    216|                                        .value(),
 1793|       |                                    Some(hyperlimit::Sign::Positive)
 1794|       |                                );
 1795|    216|                                area + Real::diff_of_products(
 1796|    216|                                    &(&b.x - &a.x),
 1797|    216|                                    &(&c.y - &a.y),
 1798|    216|                                    &(&b.y - &a.y),
 1799|    216|                                    &(&c.x - &a.x),
 1800|    216|                                )
 1801|    216|                            });
 1802|       |                    // Outer twice-area is 440; the hole's is 4 - 2*zero.
 1803|     36|                    assert_eq!(
 1804|     36|                        hyperlimit::compare_reals(
 1805|     36|                            &twice_area,
 1806|     36|                            &(Real::from(436) + Real::from(2) * &zero),
 1807|     36|                            context.predicate_policy()
 1808|       |                        )
 1809|     36|                        .value(),
 1810|       |                        Some(Ordering::Equal)
 1811|       |                    );
 1812|       |                }
 1813|       |            }
 1814|       |        }
 1815|      3|    }

/tmp/csgrs-research-closeout-checkout/crates/hypertri/src/error.rs:
    1|       |use std::fmt;
    2|       |
    3|       |/// Crate-local result type.
    4|       |pub type Result<T> = std::result::Result<T, Error>;
    5|       |
    6|       |/// Errors returned by triangulation APIs.
    7|       |#[derive(Clone, Debug, Eq, PartialEq)]
    8|       |pub enum Error {
    9|       |    /// A numeric predicate could not be decided within the configured exact
   10|       |    /// policy.
   11|       |    PredicateUndecided {
   12|       |        /// Predicate or Real decision that failed.
   13|       |        predicate: &'static str,
   14|       |    },
   15|       |    /// Input data violates the API contract.
   16|       |    InvalidInput {
   17|       |        /// Human-readable reason.
   18|       |        reason: &'static str,
   19|       |    },
   20|       |    /// The current implementation could not find an ear in a polygon ring.
   21|       |    NoEarFound,
   22|       |    /// The requested feature is not compiled or cannot be satisfied by the
   23|       |    /// active exact policy.
   24|       |    UnsupportedFeature {
   25|       |        /// Feature that is not available in the current build or policy.
   26|       |        feature: &'static str,
   27|       |    },
   28|       |}
   29|       |
   30|       |impl fmt::Display for Error {
   31|      8|    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
   32|      8|        match self {
   33|      2|            Self::PredicateUndecided { predicate } => {
   34|      2|                write!(f, "predicate could not be decided: {predicate}")
   35|       |            }
   36|      2|            Self::InvalidInput { reason } => write!(f, "invalid input: {reason}"),
   37|      2|            Self::NoEarFound => write!(f, "no valid polygon ear could be found"),
   38|      2|            Self::UnsupportedFeature { feature } => write!(f, "unsupported feature: {feature}"),
   39|       |        }
   40|      8|    }
   41|       |}
   42|       |
   43|       |impl std::error::Error for Error {}

/tmp/csgrs-research-closeout-checkout/crates/hypertri/src/f64.rs:
    1|       |//! Runtime `f64` triangulation entry points.
    2|       |
    3|       |#[cfg(feature = "cdt")]
    4|       |use crate::cdt::{ConstrainedTriangulation, DelaunayTriangulation};
    5|       |#[cfg(any(feature = "earcut", feature = "cdt"))]
    6|       |use crate::context::{TriangulationContext, TriangulationOutcome};
    7|       |#[cfg(any(feature = "earcut", feature = "cdt"))]
    8|       |use crate::error::{Error, Result};
    9|       |#[cfg(feature = "cdt")]
   10|       |use crate::types::Constraint;
   11|       |#[cfg(feature = "earcut")]
   12|       |use crate::types::TriangleIndices;
   13|       |#[cfg(any(feature = "earcut", feature = "cdt"))]
   14|       |use crate::types::{ExactPoint, Point2, Real};
   15|       |
   16|       |/// Triangulate finite `f64` polygon input after exact dyadic lifting.
   17|       |///
   18|       |/// `hyperreal::Real::try_from(f64)` decodes IEEE-754 values as exact dyadic
   19|       |/// rationals. This boundary therefore preserves the caller's finite binary
   20|       |/// input exactly instead of making topology decisions in floating arithmetic.
   21|       |#[cfg(feature = "earcut")]
   22|     10|pub fn earcut(
   23|     10|    context: &TriangulationContext,
   24|     10|    vertices: &[[f64; 2]],
   25|     10|    hole_indices: &[usize],
   26|     10|) -> Result<TriangulationOutcome<TriangleIndices>> {
   27|     10|    crate::earcut::triangulate(context, &lift_vertices(vertices)?, hole_indices)
                  ^6                                                          ^4 ^6
   28|     10|}
   29|       |
   30|       |/// Triangulate finite `f64` points after exact dyadic lifting.
   31|       |#[cfg(feature = "cdt")]
   32|      7|pub fn delaunay(
   33|      7|    context: &TriangulationContext,
   34|      7|    points: &[[f64; 2]],
   35|      7|) -> Result<TriangulationOutcome<DelaunayTriangulation>> {
   36|      7|    validate_f64_vertices(points)?;
                                               ^2
   37|      5|    let exact = lift_vertices(points)?;
                                                   ^0
   38|      5|    crate::cdt::delaunay(context, &exact)
   39|      7|}
   40|       |
   41|       |/// Triangulate finite `f64` points with the BRIO-style batch schedule after
   42|       |/// exact dyadic lifting. See [`crate::cdt::delaunay_spatial`] for tie behavior.
   43|       |#[cfg(feature = "cdt")]
   44|      4|pub fn delaunay_spatial(
   45|      4|    context: &TriangulationContext,

/tmp/csgrs-research-closeout-checkout/crates/hypertri/src/nd.rs:
    1|       |//! Exact D-dimensional triangulation storage and semantic oracles.
    2|       |//!
    3|       |//! The module provides two complementary layers. [`TriangulationDataStructureD`]
    4|       |//! stores stable vertex and cell handles with explicit finite/infinite hull
    5|       |//! semantics and report-bearing validation. [`DelaunayComplex`] is a small,
    6|       |//! exhaustive exact oracle for validation, regression tests, insertion-cavity
    7|       |//! reports, and functional bistellar flips. It preserves all certified
    8|       |//! empty-sphere simplices in cospherical cases rather than imposing an
    9|       |//! arbitrary tie break. A production mutable TDS insertion/flip scheduler is
   10|       |//! still future work.
   11|       |//!
   12|       |//! # Examples
   13|       |//!
   14|       |//! Construct a small TDS with explicit finite/infinite hull semantics and run
   15|       |//! report-bearing validation before wrapping it as a triangulation:
   16|       |//!
   17|       |//! ```
   18|       |//! use hypertri::{Cell, PointD, Real, TriangulationD, TriangulationDataStructureD};
   19|       |//!
   20|       |//! let mut tds = TriangulationDataStructureD::new(2)?;
   21|       |//! let v0 = tds.add_finite_vertex(PointD::new(vec![Real::from(0), Real::from(0)]))?;
   22|       |//! let v1 = tds.add_finite_vertex(PointD::new(vec![Real::from(1), Real::from(0)]))?;
   23|       |//! let vinf = tds.add_infinite_vertex()?;
   24|       |//! tds.add_cell(Cell::with_infinite_status(
   25|       |//!     vec![v0, v1, vinf],
   26|       |//!     vec![None, None, None],
   27|       |//!     true,
   28|       |//! ))?;
   29|       |//!
   30|       |//! let report = tds.validate_combinatorial_report();
   31|       |//! assert!(report.is_valid());
   32|       |//! assert!(tds.vertex(vinf).unwrap().is_infinite());
   33|       |//! let triangulation = TriangulationD::new(tds)?;
   34|       |//! assert_eq!(triangulation.tds().dimension(), 2);
   35|       |//! # Ok::<(), hypertri::Error>(())
   36|       |//! ```
   37|       |//!
   38|       |//! Build an exact D-dimensional Delaunay oracle, insert a point, and keep the
   39|       |//! conflict/cavity facts that justified the rebuild:
   40|       |//!
   41|       |//! ```
   42|       |//! use hypertri::{PointD, PredicatePolicy, Real, TriangulationContext};
   43|       |//! use hypertri::nd::delaunay_complex;
   44|       |//!
   45|       |//! let points = vec![
   46|       |//!     PointD::new(vec![Real::from(0), Real::from(0), Real::from(0)]),
   47|       |//!     PointD::new(vec![Real::from(1), Real::from(0), Real::from(0)]),
   48|       |//!     PointD::new(vec![Real::from(0), Real::from(1), Real::from(0)]),
   49|       |//!     PointD::new(vec![Real::from(0), Real::from(0), Real::from(1)]),
   50|       |//! ];
   51|       |//! let context = TriangulationContext::new(PredicatePolicy::STRICT);
   52|       |//! let complex = delaunay_complex(&context, &points)?.value;
   53|       |//! let inserted = PointD::new(vec![Real::from(1), Real::from(1), Real::from(1)]);
   54|       |//! let report = complex.insert_point_oracle(&context, inserted)?.value;
   55|       |//! assert_eq!(report.inserted_vertex(), 4);
   56|       |//! report.result().validate(&context)?;
   57|       |//! # Ok::<(), hypertri::Error>(())
   58|       |//! ```
   59|       |//!
   60|       |//! Validate and apply a bistellar flip on a cospherical square. The
   61|       |//! exact oracle preserves the degeneracy instead of choosing a primitive-float
   62|       |//! tie break:
   63|       |//!
   64|       |//! ```
   65|       |//! use hypertri::{
   66|       |//!     BistellarFlipD, DelaunayComplex, PointD, PredicatePolicy, Real, Simplex,
   67|       |//!     TriangulationContext,
   68|       |//! };
   69|       |//!
   70|       |//! let points = vec![
   71|       |//!     PointD::new(vec![Real::from(0), Real::from(0)]),
   72|       |//!     PointD::new(vec![Real::from(1), Real::from(0)]),
   73|       |//!     PointD::new(vec![Real::from(1), Real::from(1)]),
   74|       |//!     PointD::new(vec![Real::from(0), Real::from(1)]),
   75|       |//! ];
   76|       |//! let complex = DelaunayComplex::from_parts(
   77|       |//!     2,
   78|       |//!     points,
   79|       |//!     vec![Simplex::new(vec![0, 1, 2]), Simplex::new(vec![0, 2, 3])],
   80|       |//! );
   81|       |//! let context = TriangulationContext::new(PredicatePolicy::STRICT);
   82|       |//! complex.validate(&context)?;
   83|       |//!
   84|       |//! let flip = BistellarFlipD::new(vec![0, 1, 2, 3], vec![1, 3]);
   85|       |//! let checked = complex.validate_bistellar_flip(&context, &flip).value;
   86|       |//! assert!(checked.is_valid());
   87|       |//! let applied = complex.flip_oracle(&context, &flip)?.value;
   88|       |//! assert_eq!(applied.result().cells().len(), 2);
   89|       |//! # Ok::<(), hypertri::Error>(())
   90|       |//! ```
   91|       |//!
   92|       |//! Failed preconditions remain explicit API results:
   93|       |//!
   94|       |//! ```
   95|       |//! use hypertri::{
   96|       |//!     BistellarFlipD, DelaunayComplex, PointD, PredicatePolicy, Real, Simplex,
   97|       |//!     TriangulationContext,
   98|       |//! };
   99|       |//!
  100|       |//! let points = vec![
  101|       |//!     PointD::new(vec![Real::from(0), Real::from(0)]),
  102|       |//!     PointD::new(vec![Real::from(1), Real::from(0)]),
  103|       |//!     PointD::new(vec![Real::from(0), Real::from(1)]),
  104|       |//! ];
  105|       |//! let complex = DelaunayComplex::from_parts(
  106|       |//!     2,
  107|       |//!     points,
  108|       |//!     vec![Simplex::new(vec![0, 1, 2])],
  109|       |//! );
  110|       |//! let context = TriangulationContext::new(PredicatePolicy::STRICT);
  111|       |//! let bad = BistellarFlipD::new(vec![0, 1, 2], vec![0]);
  112|       |//! let report = complex.validate_bistellar_flip(&context, &bad).value;
  113|       |//! assert_eq!(report.reason(), Some("D-dimensional flip circuit has wrong arity"));
  114|       |//! ```
  115|       |

 1364|       |
 1365|       |/// Report from oracle-backed insertion into a D-dimensional Delaunay complex.
 1366|       |///
 1367|       |/// This is not yet the production TDS cavity-rebuild algorithm. It is a
 1368|       |/// certified construction report for the semantic oracle: conflicts are

 1831|       |/// Build an exact D-dimensional Delaunay complex by exhaustive predicates.
 1832|       |///
 1833|       |/// This routine is deliberately small and exact. It enumerates all
 1834|       |/// `dimension + 1` point subsets, rejects affinely dependent subsets with an
 1835|       |/// exact orientation determinant, then applies the Delaunay empty-sphere test
 1836|       |/// with an exact lifted determinant. It is appropriate for validation,
 1837|       |/// regression tests, and small scientific inputs. Large production
 1838|       |/// D-dimensional workloads should use a mutable TDS/flip pipeline and treat
 1839|       |/// this exhaustive path as a semantic oracle.
 1840|    107|pub fn delaunay_complex(
 1841|    107|    context: &TriangulationContext,
 1842|    107|    points: &[PointD],
 1843|    107|) -> Result<TriangulationOutcome<DelaunayComplex>> {
 1844|    107|    let evaluator = PredicateEvaluator::new(context);
 1845|    107|    let complex = delaunay_complex_inner(&evaluator, points)?;
                      ^95                                                 ^12
 1846|     95|    Ok(evaluator.finish(complex))
 1847|    107|}
 1848|       |

 2011|  5.76k|fn insphere_inside_sign(dimension: usize, orientation: Sign) -> Sign {

 2748|      2|            "D-dimensional orientation",
 2749|      2|            "D-dimensional in-sphere",
 2750|      2|        ] {

 2752|       |        }

 2754|      2|    }

    1|       |//! Shared polygon input normalization.
    2|       |//!
    3|       |//! Polygon triangulation algorithms should consume this module rather than
    4|       |//! reading hole indices independently. The separation keeps region semantics
    5|       |//! explicit and prevents algorithms from interpreting the same flat input
    6|       |//! differently.
    7|       |
    8|       |use crate::error::{Error, Result};
    9|       |#[cfg(any(feature = "earcut", all(feature = "cdt", feature = "runtime-select")))]
   10|       |use crate::predicate_evaluator::PredicateEvaluator;
   11|       |use crate::types::ExactPoint;
   12|       |
   13|       |/// A borrowed ring over a flat polygon vertex buffer.
   14|       |#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, Eq, PartialEq)]
   15|       |pub struct RingRange {
   16|       |    /// First vertex index in the flat input.
   17|       |    pub start: usize,
   18|       |    /// One-past-the-end vertex index in the flat input.
   19|       |    pub end: usize,
   20|       |}
   21|       |
   22|       |impl RingRange {
   23|       |    /// Number of vertices in the ring range.
   24|  30.9k|    pub const fn len(self) -> usize {
   25|  30.9k|        self.end - self.start
   26|  30.9k|    }
   27|       |
   28|       |    /// Returns true when the ring has no vertices.
   29|      2|    pub const fn is_empty(self) -> bool {
   30|      2|        self.start == self.end
   31|      2|    }
   32|       |}
   33|       |
   34|       |/// Validated borrowed polygon rings.
   35|       |#[derive(Clone, Debug, Eq, PartialEq)]
   36|       |pub struct PolygonRings {
   37|       |    exterior: RingRange,
   38|       |    holes: Vec<RingRange>,
   39|       |}
   40|       |
   41|       |impl PolygonRings {
   42|       |    /// Exterior ring range.

    3|       |use crate::context::{TriangulationContext, TriangulationOutcome};

   10|       |/// Polygon triangulation algorithm requested at runtime.
   11|       |#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, Eq, PartialEq)]
   12|       |pub enum PolygonTriangulationAlgorithm {
   13|       |    /// Select an available algorithm from input shape and options.
   14|       |    Auto,
   15|       |    /// Use the earcut-style polygon algorithm.
   16|       |    #[cfg(feature = "earcut")]
   17|       |    Earcut,
   18|       |    /// Use the constrained-Delaunay path.
   19|       |    #[cfg(feature = "cdt")]

   21|       |}
   22|       |
   23|       |/// Triangle quality preference used by [`TriangulationOptions`].
   24|       |#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, Eq, PartialEq)]
   25|       |pub enum QualityPolicy {
   26|       |    /// Prefer preserving the input polygon boundary with minimal extra work.
   27|       |    PreserveBoundary,
   28|       |    /// Prefer Delaunay-like triangle quality when that algorithm is compiled.
   29|       |    PreferDelaunay,
   30|       |}
   31|       |
   32|       |/// Runtime options shared by polygon triangulation entry points.
   33|       |#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, Eq, PartialEq)]
   34|       |pub struct TriangulationOptions {
   35|       |    /// Algorithm requested by the caller.
   36|       |    pub algorithm: PolygonTriangulationAlgorithm,
   37|       |    /// Quality preference used by `Auto`.
   38|       |    pub quality: QualityPolicy,
   39|       |}
   40|       |
   41|       |impl Default for TriangulationOptions {

   43|    276|        Self {
   44|    276|            algorithm: PolygonTriangulationAlgorithm::Auto,
   45|    276|            quality: QualityPolicy::PreserveBoundary,
   46|    276|        }
   47|    276|    }
   48|       |}

   50|       |/// Runtime selection report for one completed polygon triangulation.

   53|       |/// polygon facts that justified selection. These facts are advisory scheduling
   54|       |/// metadata only; exact predicates inside the selected algorithm remain the

   56|       |#[derive(Clone, Debug, Eq, PartialEq)]

   62|       |    /// Structural facts used during runtime selection.

   64|       |}
   65|       |

  205|    260|fn resolve_algorithm_for_facts(
  206|    260|    options: TriangulationOptions,
  207|    260|    facts: &PolygonInputFacts,
  208|    260|) -> Result<PolygonTriangulationAlgorithm> {
  209|    260|    match options.algorithm {
  210|    256|        PolygonTriangulationAlgorithm::Auto => resolve_auto_algorithm(options.quality, Some(facts)),
  211|       |        #[cfg(any(feature = "earcut", feature = "cdt"))]
  212|      4|        algorithm => Ok(algorithm),

  216|    287|fn resolve_auto_algorithm(
  217|    287|    quality: QualityPolicy,
  218|    287|    facts: Option<&PolygonInputFacts>,
  219|    287|) -> Result<PolygonTriangulationAlgorithm> {
  220|       |    // Structural-dispatch note: `Auto` consumes only facts already retained on
  221|       |    // `PolygonInput`; it does not probe primitive coordinates or run topology
  222|       |    // predicates early. Degenerate/unknown-zero edges, uncertified ring
  223|       |    // orientation, and unknown local turn consistency are conservative reasons
  224|       |    // to keep the boundary-preserving earcut path when it is available, because
  225|       |    // the CDT route has to materialize every ring edge as a constraint before
  226|       |    // legalization. This is advisory scheduling, not a correctness
  227|       |    // certificate; the selected algorithm still owns exact orientation and
  228|       |    // containment predicates.
  229|    287|    let boundary_cleanup_preferred = facts.is_some_and(|facts| {
                                                                             ^256
  230|    256|        facts.known_degenerate_edge_count() > 0 || facts.unknown_edge_zero_status_count() > 0
                                                                 ^252
  231|    256|    });
  232|       |    #[cfg(not(all(feature = "cdt", feature = "earcut")))]
  233|    162|    let _ = boundary_cleanup_preferred;
  234|    287|    let algorithm = match quality {
                                        ^168
  235|       |        #[cfg(all(feature = "cdt", feature = "earcut"))]
  236|      4|        QualityPolicy::PreferDelaunay if boundary_cleanup_preferred => {
  237|      4|            PolygonTriangulationAlgorithm::Earcut
  238|       |        }
  239|       |        #[cfg(feature = "cdt")]
  240|      3|        QualityPolicy::PreferDelaunay => PolygonTriangulationAlgorithm::ConstrainedDelaunay,
  241|       |        #[cfg(feature = "earcut")]
  242|    166|        QualityPolicy::PreserveBoundary => PolygonTriangulationAlgorithm::Earcut,
  243|       |        #[cfg(all(feature = "earcut", not(feature = "cdt")))]
  244|      1|        QualityPolicy::PreferDelaunay => PolygonTriangulationAlgorithm::Earcut,
  245|       |        #[cfg(all(feature = "cdt", not(feature = "earcut")))]
  246|     46|        QualityPolicy::PreserveBoundary => PolygonTriangulationAlgorithm::ConstrainedDelaunay,
  247|       |        #[allow(unreachable_patterns)]
  248|     67|        _ => PolygonTriangulationAlgorithm::Auto,
  249|       |    };
  250|    287|    if algorithm == PolygonTriangulationAlgorithm::Auto {
  251|     67|        Err(crate::Error::UnsupportedFeature {
  252|     67|            feature: "compiled polygon triangulation algorithm",
  253|     67|        })
  254|       |    } else {
  255|    220|        Ok(algorithm)
  256|       |    }
